# Decision Register — every Product Owner decision

> **The permanent record of what has been decided.** Established 2026-08-04, on the product owner's approval of the [Knowledge Continuity Framework](architecture/423-knowledge-continuity-framework.md).
>
> **Human-authored. Never generated.** A ruling is not derivable from the repository — it is a fact about a conversation, and this is the only place it is written down.

## Why this exists

**[`DECISION_PACKS.md`](DECISION_PACKS.md) states its own rule:** *"a **staging area, not a record** — a pack is deleted when its decision is taken, and the answer lives in the ADR or document it produced."*

**That is correct for packs, and it means rulings left no index.** An ADR records an *architectural* decision; **most Product Owner rulings are not ADRs** — a deferral, a partial approval, a scope ruling, an ownership resolution. **Three of the nine rulings on 2026-08-04 were deferrals, which produce no ADR and would otherwise leave no repository trace at all.**

> **A deferral is a decision.** *"Do not force a module boundary until implementation naturally reaches M14b"* binds as firmly as a ratification.

## What a row is, and is not

| | |
|---|---|
| **A row is** | The decision, its date, its status, the modules it affects, and its repository impact |
| **A row is not** | The deliberation. **That lives in the ADR, pack or document the row names** — never here |
| **A row is never deleted** | **Superseded is a status, not a removal.** A decision that was later reversed stays, with the decision that reversed it named |

**Status vocabulary — the five states of `CLAUDE.md` §20e, plus two:**

`APPROVED` · `DEFERRED` · `PARTIALLY APPROVED` · `SUPERSEDED` · `WITHDRAWN` — and for each, whether it is **`MERGED`** to `integration`, because **a decision is authoritative when ruled and actionable when recorded.**

---

The problem is uncontrolled task selection."* **This is [432](architecture/432-central-assignment-assessment.md)'s Constrained Pull with the eligibility set made explicit and reviewable** — and it is better, because the *plan* becomes reviewable rather than only the *claim*. **The queue is a projection, never an origin**: `BACKLOG.md` stays the product owner's order, the queue holds ids only, and state stays with the lane. **New state `ASSIGNED`.** **6 of 7 requested automations are fully mechanical**; dependency resequencing is partial and deferred — dependencies are prose today | **`READY FOR PRODUCT OWNER`** | `lane-c-sync` | All lanes | 🔴 **The escape hatch in §8 is not optional**: under assigned queues, starvation stops being visible to the lane that suffers it, and a slow or absent Lane C would silently halt every implementation lane. **Measured today: Lane A has 2 READY tasks against a target of 5** |
| **D-068** | **The execution-queue governance package, prepared for review** — [434](architecture/434-execution-queue-governance-package.md). **Eight Product Office SLOs**, each with its source and the phase that makes it computable: depth ≥5 · empty-queue ≤0 cycles · replenishment ≤1 cycle · starvation at depth <3 · rolling average ≥5 · queue ageing ≤5 cycles · **≥90% of cycles above target** · escape-hatch invocations 0. **Running below target is a finding against Lane C by name, never against the lane that starved.** **The escape hatch is bounded five ways** and emits an `Escape-Hatch:` **commit trailer** — a by-product of doing the thing rather than a second action that can be skipped. **The dashboard gains 13 items, 9 computed and 4 marked `«judgement»`.** **Phase 4 is gated on five conditions over five cycles and is not scheduled** | **`READY FOR PRODUCT OWNER`** | `lane-c-sync` | All lanes | **Queue depth is the single quantity that satisfies both halves of the final principle**: at depth 1 Lane C is in the path; at depth 5 no lane decides and no lane waits. ⚠️ **Lane A is at 2 today** |

Phases 0 and 1 approved and implemented today. Phase 2 continues advisory, in observation. Phases 3 and 4 need execution evidence and are not scheduled.** **No large governance, workflow, execution-model or organizational redesign** unless a significant issue is found **during implementation**; small corrections and clarifications remain permitted. **Lane C's standing remit is now flow, not design** — backlog health, queue health, bottlenecks, package review, dependency coordination, and keeping lanes supplied | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | All lanes | **Recorded in `CLAUDE.md` §20e.** **434 is the last delivery-model initiative until execution produces evidence for another** |
## 2026-08-05 — the pipeline, the queue and the roadmap

**Five rulings, and they are about how work is prepared rather than about what is built.** *(Split from the section above on 2026-08-05: D-070 onward were dated a day later than the header claimed, and a register whose section headers disagree with their contents is the drift this file exists to prevent. **No row moved and no row changed** — only the header they sit under.)*

| # | Decision | Status | Merged | Affected | Repository impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **D-070** | **The preparation pipeline is continuous, and every eligible task is assigned.** *"Do not wait because the current queues look sufficient… If twenty tasks are independently executable, then twenty tasks should already be assigned."* **The five-slice buffer becomes a floor rather than a cap**, and a lane below it is a **Product Office finding that must name its cause** — under-preparation is this office's defect, an unanswered ruling is not, and reporting them identically hides the only one anybody can act on. **A blocked task never idles a lane that owns other work**: document, resolve, ownership unchanged, and **the lane advances to its next assigned task immediately, without waiting for Lane C.** **Thirteen pipeline-health measures appear in every report**, computed rather than asserted. ⚠️ **This does not reverse [D-060](#).** Product Office prepares **assignment and sequencing**; the owning lane still writes the implementation package and Lane C still reviews it — *"do not centrally prepare implementation packages"* stands | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | All lanes | **Recorded in [`PRODUCT_OFFICE.md`](PRODUCT_OFFICE.md) §12a–12d and `CLAUDE.md` §20e** (Task Claim Protocol steps 3/3b/4). **Queues live at [`docs/coordination/queues/`](coordination/queues/)** and now include Lane C's own. **Prompted by [C-329](LANE_COORDINATION.md)**: both lanes reported empty queues while both queue files named a current task |
| **D-071** | **Fourteen rulings in one sitting, ordered by engineering impact rather than identifier.** *"Our objective is not to close the maximum number of governance items. Our objective is to maximize engineering throughput."* **[435](architecture/435-platform-composition-rfc.md) ratified whole as [ADR-0404](architecture/adr/0404-platform-composition.md)** — all four capabilities, **the single handler shape** (*"we should not support two implementation patterns permanently"*), and *"an owner may leave; a workspace may never become ownerless; **enforce the invariant in the application layer rather than the authorization engine**."* **C-206** invitation lifecycle is Signora's — *authentication proves identity; membership, invitations and tenancy belong to Signora* · **C-208** an explicit **authenticated-but-not-member** state, **general RLS untouched** · **C-010** `apps/` is a first-class assignable module · **C-018** customer terminology lives in `explanation.py`; **business language never leaks inward** · **C-328** the forbidden dependency stays forbidden — *`commercial_profile` declares, `opportunity_engine` measures* · **C-321** Lane B's six answers adopted whole · **C-221 narrowed** — *do not expand the architecture around people until a provider capability exists; **avoid speculative architecture*** · **`execution_mode` removed from the mandatory evaluation fields** — *a field constant for every possible record carries no information*, Lane B's challenge accepted. **Closed**: C-019 · C-008 · C-205 (**responsive web is officially part of Signora; native mobile is a future roadmap item**) · C-200 · and C-012, C-013, C-021, C-207 by ADR-0404. **Deferred with triggers**: C-320 · C-322+C-002 · C-210+C-203 · C-020 · C-025 · C-005 | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | All lanes | ⚡ **Released six tasks and [TASK-901](BACKLOG.md)'s seven slices.** ⚠️ **One half remains open**: C-010 asked about `apps/` *and* `signora_platform/`, and the ruling named `apps/`. **[TASK-418](BACKLOG.md) is `BLOCKED` on an owner for `signora_platform/`**, where three of ADR-0404's four capabilities live |
| **D-072** | **Long-term roadmap decomposition is Product Office's; implementation package preparation stays the lane's.** *"I do not want Product Office to avoid decomposition because of potential staleness. The problem we had earlier was not decomposition — it was that implementation lanes were waiting for Product Office to prepare work. **Our new operating model removes that bottleneck.**"* **Decompose to Epics · Capabilities · high-level items · dependencies · milestones · architectural sequencing. Do not prepare detailed implementation slices for work far from execution** — the owning lane writes those when the initiative becomes active ([D-060](#) is untouched). **Every future initiative carries one of four classifications**: 🟦 Customer Product · 🟧 Internal Operations · 🟩 Platform/Foundation · ⬜ Architecture/Governance, **and the classification is part of backlog planning and reporting.** **"Studio" is reserved for future customer-facing experiences**; internal capabilities are **Signora Operations · Signora Control · Signora Admin.** **[TASK-426](BACKLOG.md) approved in principle** as the future single source of truth for retention, soft and hard delete, archival, restoration, GDPR deletion, legal hold, audit preservation and backup interaction — **not implemented now.** **The repository reuse policy stands**, and 438's audit is cited as validating it | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | Lane C | **Recorded in [438 §7–8](architecture/438-platform-initiatives.md).** ⚠️ **Lane C's staleness argument was too wide and is conceded there**: every example it cited — a lane name, a pair of assignees, a section count — **was detail-level. An epic-level statement does not decay that way** |

| **D-084** | **A taxonomy for reusable concepts, and a promotion test that runs downward.** **Three categories: ⚖️ Architecture Principles** *(repository-wide, **rare and stable**)* · **🏗️ Platform Capabilities** *(Matching, People, Timeline, Saved Views, Recommendation Lifecycle)* · **📐 Product Patterns** *(Stated Absence, Transparent Partial Evaluation, Predicate-Based Evaluation, Explainability)*. *"This taxonomy should help avoid promoting every repeated implementation into a new architectural principle while still preserving valuable design knowledge."* **① *"Continue preferring existing repository principles whenever they already explain the observed behaviour. A new pattern should only be introduced when existing principles cannot adequately describe recurring product semantics."*** **② Capabilities become authoritative through independent consumers, never through one appearance.** **③ Record architectural *trigger conditions* explicitly rather than recommending premature implementation.** **④ Separate concepts that share a user experience but differ in responsibility, lifecycle or ownership.** **⑤ The Domain Coverage Matrix gains category and trigger condition** — seven fields. | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | All 16 approved specifications · `CLAUDE.md` §2 · [ADR-0402](architecture/adr/0402-a-rule-is-data.md) | **[Register Part B-2](DOMAIN_DISCOVERY_REGISTER.md) adopts the three categories; [Part B-3](DOMAIN_DISCOVERY_REGISTER.md) is the pattern table; the density table gains category and trigger.** ⚠️ **Applying the promotion test to a table this office had written the previous hour *removed two rows*: supersession is fully explained by §2 and is cited rather than recorded, and action batches are a Platform Capability wearing a pattern's clothes.** **A register that only accretes stops being evidence and becomes inventory.** **[451](architecture/451-workflow-automation-traceability-audit.md) is the seventh audit**, and ⚡ **a ratified ADR answers a question [TASK-431](BACKLOG.md) has been waiting on**: [ADR-0402](architecture/adr/0402-a-rule-is-data.md) §2 makes `workflow` publish one event with *N* subscribers acting, **so approve/apply/reverse belonging to each acting module means implementing it *N* times** — **which argues for one owning context and against per-module, on ratified architecture rather than on preference.** **Six trigger conditions collected in one place for the first time**, five of which already existed scattered across documents |

| **D-086** | **Canonical Vocabulary Governance — prevention rather than detection.** *"Our objective is no longer just to detect vocabulary conflicts. ## 2026-08-06 — stewardship begins

**The first decisions taken after the discovery phase closed.** *(Dated separately because they are: `2ac3645` is the commit where the repository rolled over, and this office spent one commit dating today's work yesterday. **`state-check`'s new freshness check found it** — it reported three queues stamped 2026-08-05 against a backlog that had moved on 2026-08-06, which is the check catching a drift it was not written for.)*

| # | Decision | Status | Merged | Affected | Repository impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **D-094** | **C-333 and B-327 approved for Product Office handling, and dependency reporting gains seven mandatory fields.** **C-333** — *"investigate and correct the queue generation process. **The queue must always represent the actual executable state of the repository**… an **executable planning artifact** rather than documentation."* **Seven validations required**: `eligible_count` · `shortfall` · actual claimability · dependency satisfaction · queue freshness · `READY` items missing from queues · stale completed items remaining. **B-327** — *"reconcile every governance artifact affected by the new ICP Matching capability… **Implementation should never discover conflicting governance documentation.**"* **And the standing reporting format**: every dependency, waiting state or blocked condition carries **identifier · owner · affected tasks · current status · why it blocks · expected next action · whether Product Owner action is required.** | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | `infra/state-check.py` · all three lane queues · four governance artifacts | **The seven validations are in `state-check.py` and found four drifts on the first run — two of them in queues this office wrote.** `lane-a` queued TASK-437 while its row said `IN VALIDATION`; `lane-b` declared `shortfall = 0` against 2 of 5; `lane-c` queued TASK-418, which is with QE; ⚡ **and TASK-901 read `READY` in no lane's queue — where the finding was the *backlog*, not the queue: both lane files already listed it blocked on TASK-418, and the backlog was the optimistic one.** **Corrected to `BLOCKED` on [C-417](LANE_COORDINATION.md).** ⚠️ **The check also found a defect in its own wiring**: it sat *below* `if not findings: return 0`, so **every queue validation would have been skipped on exactly the day the rest of the repository agreed with itself** — the one day a silent drift goes unseen. **[B-327](BUGS.md) reconciles four artifacts that described ICP Matching as finished** — [443](architecture/443-account-matching-capability.md), TASK-406's row, the Register and [461](architecture/461-product-led-domain-discovery-review.md) — **so all four now say *implemented, not usable*.** ⛔ **No regression guard for B-327, deliberately**: `state-check` can compare a document to a commit, **not a claim to a codebase**, and saying so beats a check that pretends to cover it. ⚠️ **Both ids fall in Lane B's allocation block; the product owner named them, both were verified absent everywhere before use, and the collision risk is recorded for QE** |
| **D-098** | **Certification becomes a measured strategy rather than an optimisation exercise.** *"The objective is not simply faster certification… the highest confidence for the lowest justified cost."* **Ten measurement questions · a purpose-based taxonomy · certification profiles · DR execution levels · seven confidence lenses · a long-term roadmap.** ⛔ ***"Do not modify QE workflows or certification behaviour."*** **And the invariant that governs change-aware selection: *"run everything that provides additional confidence, not run as little as possible."*** | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | QE · `certification/` · `infra/` | **[465](architecture/465-certification-strategy.md), and its headline outranks [463](architecture/463-certification-architecture-assessment.md)'s.** ⚡ **Across 70 certifications, not one gate has caught a product defect.** The three failures are **two `smoke` runs stopping at stage 2 of 24 — the build — and one `mypy` gate that could not parse its own tool's output.** **All three are the gate failing to run or the environment failing to come up.** ⚠️ **And the repository already knows that distinction, for one gate only**: the release checklist says *"`make dr` aborting is not `make dr` failing… an abort means the gate never evaluated the repository"* — **a rule written for DR and implemented for nothing.** **So 463's 2.9% may be a 0% failure rate and a 2.9% abort rate, and for a risk model those mean opposite things.** **Recording a *cause* per gate — passed · failed · aborted · skipped — now outranks recording durations.** ⚡ **Two reuse findings.** **`CLAUDE.md` §20e's *"Which gates run when"* table is already a profile model** — six ratified moments — **so three of the five proposed profiles exist and are merely unmeasured, and only Release and Infrastructure are genuinely absent.** **And the Security Invariants execute twice per certification**: `testpaths` collects `tests/security_invariants` into `pytest --cov`, and the runner then re-runs them as a named gate. **The second execution buys a line in the record, not confidence — the only zero-confidence-loss saving in either document, and the gate line must stay.** ⚠️ **Change-aware selection is worth less than the brief expects**: under the stated invariant, **only four of nine gates may be selected by impact, and the five that may not include four of the cheapest.** **The expensive gates are precisely the repository-wide guarantees.** **Weakest confidence lens is the one nearest the customer** — the 1255-test frontend suite is in no gate ([B-123](BUGS.md)). ⚠️ **And this document re-tested its predecessor's central claim rather than repeating it: 463 called `smoke` the highest-value gate on a 2.9% failure rate, and the failures may mean the opposite. The number was right; the conclusion was not yet earned** |
| **D-097** | **Lane A's queue corrected, TASK-433's implementation impact reassessed, and queue integrity becomes a standing operational duty.** *"A lane should never appear to have executable work when it has none, and should never appear empty when executable work exists… **Product Office should proactively validate repository state rather than waiting for implementation lanes to identify the issue.**"* | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | All three lane queues · `infra/state-check.py` · [R-02](DOMAIN_DISCOVERY_REGISTER.md) | ⚠️ **C-333's checks passed Lane A's queue and the queue was still wrong** — **they validated `current`/`next`/`future` and never read `held` or `in_validation`.** **Check ⑧ added, and it found five drifts across two queues**: `lane-a` held **TASK-436 (`PROMOTED`)** and **TASK-903/905 (`IN VALIDATION`)**; `lane-b` held **TASK-434 and TASK-408, both `PROMOTED`** — **in a file this office had already corrected once.** **A queue is not only a list of what to take; it is a claim about everything else, and that claim was unchecked.** ✅ **On the substantive question, Lane A's queue was right**: 0 of 5 is **correct**, because the only two startable rows on the board — [TASK-201](BACKLOG.md) and [TASK-202](BACKLOG.md) — are both in Lane B's modules. **The lane is genuinely idle, and that is a Product Office capacity finding rather than a queue defect.** ⚡ **And correcting the queue produced a better argument for [TASK-433](BACKLOG.md) than any screen count**: **[R-02](DOMAIN_DISCOVERY_REGISTER.md)'s owner is `workspace_tenancy`, `modules.toml` gives `workspace_tenancy` to **Lane A**, and Lane A is the idle lane.** **So TASK-433 is not a naming question in front of sixteen screens — it is a naming question in front of the idle lane's next four prepared slices** ([464](architecture/464-task-433-implementation-impact.md)). **The recommendation is unchanged — `SavedView` adopted, `Segment` left with `commercial_profile`** — ⬜ **and the cheaper ruling remains available: *"Companies' segments are ICP cohorts"* ends the question without naming anything.** ⚠️ **The cost of delay is stated without inflation: it costs throughput, not product.** No approved screen being built today needs a saved view. **The second-order cost is the one that grows — App Shell is wave 3, all sixteen screens inherit it, and a shell built before scope has a home grows its own** |
| **D-096** | **A risk-based certification model, assessed and not implemented.** *"The objective of certification is confidence, not maximum execution time… certification should become risk-driven, not time-driven."* **P0–P3 tiers · a Certification Manifest · trigger-based selection · a Disaster Recovery split · execution metrics.** ⛔ ***"Do not implement any certification changes at this stage."*** | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | QE · `infra/` · `certification/` | **[463](architecture/463-certification-architecture-assessment.md) is the assessment, measured against 70 recorded runs — and three findings reshape the brief.** ⚡ **① Disaster Recovery is already outside `make certify`**: `REQUIRED_GATES` is `{pytest, mypy, tsc, import-linter, security-invariants, smoke}` and **DR is not among them.** It runs because a runbook has a tick-box, **not because the certification command invokes it** — so the optimisation available is real and smaller than the brief assumes. ⚠️ **② DR's result is in no certification record.** Six gate names have ever been written to the manifest and none is DR, **while [ADR-0032](architecture/adr/0032-release-certification.md) calls that file durable evidence that a commit satisfied the repository's guarantees.** A guarantee run every cycle and evidenced nowhere is a governance defect, not a performance one. ⚡ **③ The failure data inverts the intuition.** **`smoke` 2/70 · `mypy` 1/70 · `pytest` 0/70 across 3653 tests · Security Invariants 0/70 across 389.** **The two most expensive gates have never failed at certification — because §19 makes the lanes run them first — and the only gate that fails is the one a lane *cannot* run, since `smoke` binds ports 3000 and 8000.** **So value at certification time is not a gate's importance; it is the probability it catches something the lane could not.** **Recommendation: adopt the tier vocabulary, the suite manifest and trigger selection; demote nothing; and record a duration per gate first** — one field in a record that already exists, **which unblocks six of the nine metrics and without which every argument in the brief rests on an unmeasured cost.** ⛔ **Do not demote `pytest` or the Security Invariants** — a 0% failure rate is §19 working, and a gate removed is an incentive removed. ⛔ **No scheduler**: §20c-2. ⚠️ **[B-123](BUGS.md) precedes the whole plan** — the frontend suite is in no gate at all, and tiering a suite that never runs would be arranging validations that do not happen. ⚠️ **And the assessment corrected its own measurement**: the first pass reported zero failures because it read `gates` as a dict when it is a list, **so a finding that would have argued for demoting every gate came from a bug in the tool that produced it** |

Adopt Better Auth's *identity* plugins and none of its authorization.** ⛔ **Hybrid is the worst of the three, and §6 asked to be told that plainly.** 📁 **The measurement that reframes the question: Signora is using perhaps five percent of Casbin.** **`model.conf` is four equalities, `policy.csv` is 53 lines, every line uses `dom = "*"`, and no role hierarchy is configured.** ⚡ **What is deployed is a role→(resource-type, action) matrix — a CSV lookup, not a policy engine.** 🌐 **And Better Auth's organization plugin is the same shape, by its own documentation**: ***"Permissions apply to the entire organization, not individual resources or accounts"*** and ***"No stated permission inheritance or hierarchical role structures."*** ⛔ **So swapping one for the other is lateral — it buys nothing and costs a migration.** ⚡ **Sorted by what they demand, §1's twenty-three capabilities collapse to four: a non-human principal · object-level permission · context-aware and temporary permission · hierarchy.** ⛔ **Better Auth's plugin cannot express three of the four and says so; Casbin expresses all four through `model.conf`, with no library change and no migration.** 🔴 **And the finding that reframes the comparison entirely: `AuthenticatedPrincipal` carries `user_id`, `session_id`, `email`, `email_verified`, `active_workspace_id`, `token_id` — every field human — and `grep` for `service_account`, `api_key` or `agent_id` outside tests returns nothing.** ⚡ **The binding constraint on the next decade is not the policy engine; it is that Signora has no model of a non-human caller, and *neither option supplies one*.** ⛔ **Choosing on *"which does roles better"* optimises the wrong variable.** ⛔ **Hybrid rejected for a specific reason rather than complexity: it creates two role stores — `organizationRole` in Better Auth's schema and `membership.role` under RLS — which is [§2](../CLAUDE.md)'s two-representations defect *inside the security layer*, where drift is a vulnerability rather than an inconvenience.** ⚠️ **And two tenancy units: Signora's is the workspace, Better Auth's is the organization, and §1's own vision has organizations *containing* workspaces.** 🌐 **A third architecture named honestly rather than omitted: Zanzibar / OpenFGA / SpiceDB.** ✅ **It is the industry answer to object-level permission at scale and answers *"which accounts may this seller see?"* from the store.** ⛔ **Not recommended today — [§3](../CLAUDE.md) Principle 6 permits one PostgreSQL database, OpenFGA is a service, and Signora already answers that question with Row-Level Security.** ⚡ **Recorded so it is a considered option the day per-object sharing arrives, not a discovery.** 🧠 **⑪, as Chief Architect: keep Casbin, adopt none of its authorization, and spend the saved effort on a principal model that admits non-humans and on object-level permission.** ⚡ **The hard-to-reverse decision is the *tenancy* one, not the engine one** — **adopting the organization model imports its unit of tenancy into every check.** ⚠️ **Casbin's value here is optionality rather than features: it is doing almost nothing, and every future demand is a text-file change.** ⛔ **What to avoid even though it looks simpler: the organization plugin would delete 53 lines of CSV and feel like a simplification, while fixing the model at flat and organization-scoped — which is how a repository arrives at hybrid by accident rather than by choice.** ✅ **And the strongest argument is the product's own: Signora's differentiator is agents acting inside a customer's data.** **Expressing *"the agent may read but not spend"* — `Resource.RESEARCH` already exists for exactly that reason — is one policy line in a model that admits non-human subjects, and impossible in one that only admits members** |
| **D-176** | ⚡ **Authorization ratification — final due diligence.** **Design the long-term principal model across nine identity kinds · draw the Better Auth / Signora boundary explicitly · assess whether Casbin reaches maturity · evaluate migration risk in both directions · research Better Auth's trajectory · ⛔ ***"attempt to disprove 'Casbin only'"*** · and bring unasked architectural recommendations.** **After this, the principal model, authorization model, identity boundary, tenancy boundary and platform split are ratified and not revisited without materially superior evidence** | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | [495](architecture/495-principal-and-identity-boundary.md) · [494](architecture/494-authorization-architecture.md) · **every security implementation** | ✅ **§6 answered first because it decides ratification: three attempts to disprove *Casbin only*, two failed and one partially succeeded.** ⛔ **Attack ① — *there are already two evaluators, because authorization must also happen in Next.js* — FAILS.** 📁 **`frontend/lib/auth/jwt.ts:85` already refuses: *"Deliberately carries no permission or capability claims. Casbin is the single source of authorization truth."*** ⚡ **The architecture closed it before it was attacked, which is a better reason to ratify than the original argument.** ⛔ **Attack ② — *object-level at scale will force a replacement* — fails as an argument against Casbin: Signora answers *which rows may this caller see* with RLS, not the policy engine.** ⚠️ **Attack ③ — *custom roles need a database adapter, so "no migration" is false* — PARTIALLY SUCCEEDS, and [D-175](DECISION_REGISTER.md)'s claim is corrected rather than defended.** 📁 **`policy.csv` is a file in this repository and a customer cannot commit to it.** ⛔ **One storage migration is certain under every option.** ✅ **The recommendation survives because the same requirement under Better Auth is `dynamicAccessControl` plus its `organizationRole` table — the same migration, into a model that then cannot express the other three demands.** ⚡ **§1 delivered as a design rather than a list: one principal type with a `kind`, a `subject_id`, a non-nullable `workspace_id` for every machine kind, an `owner_user_id` so revocation cascades on offboarding — and `acting_for`, the field the model exists for.** ✅ **And the rule that makes agents safe is one line: *a principal's effective permission is the INTERSECTION of its own grant and the grant of the principal it acts for*.** 📁 **`Resource.RESEARCH` exists because *"reading an account is free and asking for research spends a model call"* — intersection is what expresses *"the agent may read but not spend"* with no second policy language.** ⛔ **Union would be a privilege-escalation primitive.** ⚠️ **Better Auth reaches for the weaker version — intersection at *grant* time; evaluation-time is correct, because a human's permissions can be reduced after an agent is created.** ✅ **§2's boundary in one sentence: Better Auth answers *who is this*, Signora answers *what may they do here*** — ⛔ **and every capability needing the word *here* is Signora's, because Better Auth's unit is the organization and Signora's is the workspace.** ⚡ **§4's answer is that the preparation is already done: the enforcer is a single port and the `Resource` vocabulary is Signora's, so an OpenFGA move would be a swap rather than a rewrite.** 🧠 **That the required preparation already exists is the strongest evidence the boundary is drawn in the right place.** 🔴 **And §7's most useful unasked finding: the real extensibility constraint is `Resource`, not Casbin** — **15 members in a frozen package, needing an enum edit and a policy line in two files for every new capability.** ✅ **Plus: make the intersection rule a Security Invariant *before* the first agent exists, because it is cheap now and unwritable afterwards** |
| **D-177** | ⚡ **The final security architecture — a one-time foundational decision, answered as Chief Architect rather than as Product Office.** ⛔ ***"The objective is not to determine whether Casbin is currently better than Better Auth"*** — **but the single architecture serving millions of users, thousands of enterprises, autonomous agents, marketplace extensions and an API-first future.** **Research beyond the repository · evaluate every layer separately · justify every rejection · ⚡ think AI-first · then attempt to invalidate the result** | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | [496](architecture/496-security-architecture-blueprint.md) · [`workflows.toml`](coordination/workflows.toml) · **every security implementation** | 🔴 **The blueprint turns on one idea, and it invalidates the framing of every previous package including this office's own: *authorization is not one question, it is three*.** **① Capability — *may this kind of principal do this kind of thing* — a small static matrix, microseconds, in-process. ② Relationship — *may this principal touch this specific object* — a graph, a store. ③ ⚡ Quota — *may this principal spend this* — a counter, a limit, a window.** ⛔ **RBAC, ABAC and ReBAC all answer boolean questions, and *every product [D-177](DECISION_REGISTER.md) names answers only the first two*.** 🔴 **So the most important finding is that an AI-native platform needs a *quantitative* authorization dimension that no vendor supplies — and it is therefore Signora's by definition.** 📁 **The repository already circles it without naming it: `Resource.RESEARCH` exists because *"asking for research spends a model call"*, `TASK-424` is *"a workspace has an AI budget"*, and `TASK-411`/`412` are usage accounting.** 🌐 **Research validated the split rather than a library: OpenFGA is CNCF Zanzibar with `list-objects`; SpiceDB is gRPC with strong consistency and *OpenAI runs it for ChatGPT Enterprise connectors at tens of billions of permissions* — the closest analogue to Signora that exists; Cedar is formally verified and 28–35× faster than OpenFGA.** ⚡ **The pattern behind every large platform's choice is that they *split the layers* rather than picking one engine.** ✅ **Recommendation: Better Auth owns identity and never authorization. Signora owns tenancy, principals, audit and risk permanently. Authorization is three questions behind one port** — **capability by Casbin today and Cedar if verification is ever bought; relationship by Row-Level Security today and OpenFGA when a design first shows one person sharing one record; quota built, because nobody sells it.** ⚡ **AI-first produced three properties no off-the-shelf model has: *attenuation* — authority may only narrow along an agent chain, which is capability-security thinking and one matcher clause, and is unrepresentable in a role model because roles compose by *union*; *quota*, checked before the act and denied in the same shape as a permission; and *attribution*, where every act names both `subject_id` and `acting_for` — an audit line naming only the agent cannot answer who authorised it, and one naming only the human is a lie.** ⚠️ **The invalidation attempt produced one attack that lands hardest, and it is against this office: *deferring OpenFGA is the same mistake as deferring the design tokens — identified, published, never scheduled, and a lane paid for it*.** ✅ **The mitigation is structural rather than a promise: the three reopening triggers are now watched in [`workflows.toml`](coordination/workflows.toml) where they are read every cycle, not in a document nobody re-opens.** 🧠 **The closing judgement: the decision that matters is not which engine — it is the port, the workspace as the tenancy unit, and a principal model where authority only ever narrows.** ⛔ **Those three make every later engine choice reversible, which is the only honest way to make a ten-year decision** |
| **D-178** | ⚡ **Commodity versus differentiation — a build-versus-reuse philosophy for the whole product, not only security.** **Classify every capability as Commodity · Platform · Domain · evaluate *maturity* rather than functionality · research beyond the current options · justify every build AND every reuse · then attempt to invalidate the result** | `APPROVED` | `lane-c-sync` | [497](architecture/497-commodity-and-differentiation.md) · [`09-build-vs-buy.md`](architecture/09-build-vs-buy.md) · **every dependency decision** | ⚡ **The philosophy already exists, ratified, with the exact test §1 proposes.** 📁 **`09-build-vs-buy.md` — *Living document — Architectural Constitution — RATIFIED* — says *"Build it only if it is Signora's competitive advantage. Buy it if a mature vendor already solves it well."*** ⛔ **So [497](architecture/497-commodity-and-differentiation.md) grades the doctrine rather than writing a second one — a second philosophy beside a ratified one is the two-authorities defect this office has reported four times this week.** 🔴 **And the grading found something: eight of twelve ratified *buy* decisions are unadopted, and in three cases a workflow is blocked or a lane built a workaround while the vendor sits ratified and unbought.** **Notifications — Novu ratified, Workflow 17 blocked until yesterday on notification *delivery infrastructure*, which `09` already says not to build.** **Email — Resend ratified, and [TASK-474](BACKLOG.md)'s developer mailbox exists because nothing is wired, gating Workflows 3, 4 and 5.** **Search — Meilisearch ratified, Workflow 16 undecomposed.** ⚡ **The pattern, and it is [TASK-490](BACKLOG.md)'s exactly: a ratified-but-unbought decision behaves like an *unmade* one.** **The answer existed, in a document, and no row carried it — so a lane reaches the capability and builds around it.** ⚡ **The doctrine's real gap is granularity, and it is what §4 asks to fix.** **`09` has one row per *product*; *"Authorization → Casbin"* is a single row covering three questions.** ⛔ **Capability authorization is correctly bought; relationship authorization was bought *without noticing* — it is PostgreSQL RLS; and quota authorization has no vendor at all and was never classified.** ⚠️ **A product-level row can hide a capability with no vendor, which is how *"we buy authorization"* coexisted with an unclassified build.** ✅ **Five capabilities newly classified as Domain: AI authorization · AI budgets and quota · agent delegation · risk signals · workspace security analytics.** **They share one property — they are *authorization about AI*, and no vendor sells them because no vendor has the domain.** ✅ **And the finding that should reassure rather than alarm: every one of the thirteen *build* decisions has aged well and none has been regretted.** ⛔ **The doctrine's failure mode is not over-building. It is under-buying — deciding to buy, recording it, and then meeting the capability again in a workflow that has no idea the decision was already made.** ✅ **Recommendation: amend `09` rather than replace it — one row per *capability*, three categories instead of two, and an *adopted* column** — **and treat the three blocked cases as rows rather than findings** |

## How this register is maintained

**Lane C records a row when the product owner rules.** The row names the decision, never restates the deliberation.

**Two things must be true of every row and are checkable:**

> **The `Merged` column says where the ruling is recorded, not where it was made.** A row reading `lane-c-sync` means the decision is **authoritative and not yet actionable** — `CLAUDE.md` §20e's five states, and the distinction Lane A's challenge established.
>
> **A superseded row is amended, never removed.** D-014 stays, marked `SUPERSEDED by D-022`, because *"a record of what was true then must not be rewritten"* (`CLAUDE.md` §2) — and because the reason a decision was reversed is usually more useful than the reversal.

**Identifiers are permanent and sequential.** `D-nnn` is never reused. **They are not per-lane blocks**, because only the product owner creates one.
